Um... well, I guess I do, a little bit? But I think it's mostly just me trying to be thorough, you know? Like, before I leave the house, or before I leave my classroom for the day, I’ll... Um... I wouldn't say I think something *terrible* is going to happen, exactly. It's more just... well, like I was saying before, I just want to be really responsible, you know?

Um... yeah, I guess I do. It's actually kind of why I decided to come in and talk to someone. It's not like crazy voices or anything scary like that, I promise! It's just... these really nagging thoughts that I forgot something important.

It's a little embarrassing to admit that I can't just brush it off like a normal person would. I really try to ignore it, but it's just so persistent sometimes. Does that sound like what you were asking about? I hope I explained that okay.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, well... I guess it kind of goes back to what I was saying before, about how I check things? I'm sorry to keep bringing up the same stuff, I hope that's okay! 

But, um, I wouldn't call them "rituals," really. That sounds so... I don't know, intense? It's just my little routine. Like, before I leave the house for work, or before I lock up my classroom at the end of the day, I just have this way I like to go over things. I'll check my aunt's pill organizer, make sure the stove is off, look over my lesson plans... or sometimes I just have to silently replay what I did in my head, just to be absolutely sure. 

I don't necessarily have a strict, rigid order I *have* to do them in, but I do feel like I need to keep checking or replaying it until I feel... settled, I guess? Until I feel calm enough to actually walk out the door. I know it's a little silly, especially since it makes me run late sometimes, which I feel terrible about! I really try not to let it affect my schedule. But I just like to be thorough. Is that... is that the kind of thing you're looking for?

Um, well... I wouldn't say they're causing a *lot* of distress. I really don't want to make it sound worse than it is, because I know there are people dealing with much harder things!

I really don't want to make it sound worse than it is, because I know there are people dealing with much harder things! \n\nI'm still getting through everything I need to do, you know? I always try to put on a happy face for them, though!\n\nAnd, um, my lesson prep and even just my evening journaling... they just don't feel quite as smooth or relaxing as they used to. It's just a little harder to wind down. But I'm still functioning fine, I promise! I just wanted to see if there was a way to make things a little easier. Does that make sense? Um, yeah, I suppose that's a good way to put it. I really don't want to sound like I'm paranoid or anything! I know the chances of something going wrong are super small. 

But, um, yeah, when I go back over things—like checking my aunt's pill organizer, or making sure the stove is off, or looking through my lesson plans before I leave the classroom—it's mostly just to be absolutely sure everything is safe and taken care of. Or, you know, when I silently replay what I did in my head. I just want to prevent any little mistakes that could cause a problem for my aunt or my students. I'd feel just awful if something happened because I wasn't careful enough!

So I just do my little routine until I feel calm enough to move on. Even though, like I said, it does make me run late sometimes, which I'm really trying to work on! I just like to be responsible. I hope that doesn't sound too strange?

It's actually kind of why I decided to come in and talk to someone. It's not like crazy voices or anything scary like that, I promise! It's just... these really nagging thoughts that I forgot something important. \n\nLike, I'll be driving to work and suddenly I just can't stop thinking, \"Did I leave my aunt's medicine out wrong?\" or \"Did I set up my classroom materials safely so the kids won't get hurt?\" And the thing is, I *know* I probably handled it. I really try to just push the thoughts away because, like I said, I know I probably handled everything fine. But the doubt just sneaks right back in, completely uninvited. I really wish they wouldn't do that, because it makes me feel like I'm not doing a good enough job, you know? I hope it doesn't make me sound like I'm a scattered person, because I really do try my best to be organized! Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, well, I guess... yes, sometimes? I mean, I wouldn't say I'm obsessed with it or anything! I just... I like to be thorough. 

So, um, yeah, before I leave the house in the morning, or before I lock up my classroom at the end of the day, I will kind of... go back over things. Like, I'll check my aunt's pill organizer a few times, or make sure the stove is off, or look through my lesson plans again. And if I can't physically check it, sometimes I just... silently replay what I did in my head, just to be sure I did it right. 

I just do it until I feel calm enough to move on, you know? It's really just a way to make sure everything is taken care of. I mean, I know I mentioned it makes me run a little late sometimes, which I'm definitely trying to fix! But it's just my way of being responsible. I hope that doesn't sound too strange or... or like I'm doing something wrong?

Um, well... I guess maybe a little bit? I know I brought this up a minute ago, about how I check things before I leave the house or my classroom. When I go back over those same things—you know, my aunt's pill organizer, the stove, my lesson plans—I do kind of like to do it in a certain routine.

I mean, I know I just mentioned that it makes me run late sometimes, which I'm really trying to work on because I absolutely hate inconveniencing my coworkers or my students! But... yeah, doing all that just helps me feel settled so I can focus on being a good teacher. \n\nI hope that's the kind of answer you were looking for? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Are these worries and checking behaviors affecting your relationships with others or your ability to teach and work effectively?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I wouldn't say they're ruining anything, you know? I'm definitely still getting through all my work commitments and taking care of my family just fine. I try really hard not to let it affect anyone else. \n\nBut... I guess, if I'm being completely honest, it does make my mornings take a lot longer than they probably should. I'll sometimes get to school feeling all rushed and... just kind of behind before the kids even get there, which I really hate. And, um, things like my lesson prep, or even just my journaling in the evening—which usually helps me unwind—they just don't feel quite as smooth or relaxing as they used to be. \n\nIt's nothing I can't handle, I promise! I'm still doing my best. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is... I just want to give you the whole picture.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to perform these checking behaviors or else something bad will happen?\n\nPatient: Um... I wouldn't say I think something *terrible* is going to happen, exactly. It's more just... well, like I was saying before, I just want to be really responsible, you know? \n\nSo, before I leave the house, or before I lock up my classroom, I'll just go back over those things a few times. You know, checking the pill organizer, making sure the stove is off, looking at my lesson plans... or just silently replaying what I did in my head. I just... I feel like I *have* to do it until I feel calm enough to actually move on. If I don't, I just feel this... this nagging feeling that I missed something important, and I wouldn't want to let anyone down because I wasn't careful enough. \n\nI know I mentioned it makes me run late sometimes, which I'm really trying to fix! But I just do it to be safe. Is that... is that normal? I hope that answers your question okay.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these checking behaviors are necessary to prevent something bad from happening to yourself or others?\n\nPatient: Um, I know I'm probably sounding like a broken record right now, I'm so sorry! But... well, no, I don't really think of it as preventing a huge disaster or someone getting hurt, exactly. It's really just... like I was saying, before I leave the house or my classroom, I'll just go back over those same things a few times. You know, checking the pill organizer, the stove, my lesson plans, or just silently replaying what I did. I just do it until I feel calm enough to move on. \n\nI know I keep mentioning that it makes me run late sometimes, and I promise I'm really trying to get better at that because I hate being an inconvenience to anyone! But it's mostly just about wanting to be a thorough, responsible person, not because I think someone is in actual danger. \n\nI hope it's okay that my answer is basically the same as before? I just want to make sure I'm answering you right and giving you exactly what you need.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any thoughts that keep repeating in your mind, even when you try to ignore them or think about something else?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess I do. It's actually kind of why I decided to come in and talk to someone. It's not like crazy voices or anything scary like that, I promise! It's just... these really nagging thoughts that I forgot something important. \n\nLike, I'll be driving to work and suddenly I just can't stop thinking, \"Did I leave my aunt's medicine out wrong?\" or \"Did I set up my classroom materials safely so the kids won't get hurt?\" And the thing is, I *know* I probably handled it. I'm usually so careful! But even when I try to just push it away and focus on my day, the doubt just... keeps popping back into my head. \n\nIt's a little embarrassing to admit that I can't just brush it off like a normal person would. I really try to ignore it, but it's just so persistent sometimes. Does that sound like what you were asking about? I hope I explained that okay.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel compelled to perform any specific rituals or behaviors in a specific order or manner?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess it kind of goes back to what I was saying before, about how I check things? I'm sorry to keep bringing up the same stuff, I hope that's okay! \n\nBut, um, I wouldn't call them \"rituals,\" really. That sounds so... I don't know, intense? It's just my little routine. Like, before I leave the house for work, or before I lock up my classroom at the end of the day, I just have this way I like to go over things. I'll check my aunt's pill organizer, make sure the stove is off, look over my lesson plans... or sometimes I just have to silently replay what I did in my head, just to be absolutely sure. \n\nI don't necessarily have a strict, rigid order I *have* to do them in, but I do feel like I need to keep checking or replaying it until I feel... settled, I guess? Until I feel calm enough to actually walk out the door. I know it's a little silly, especially since it makes me run late sometimes, which I feel terrible about! I really try not to let it affect my schedule. But I just like to be thorough. Is that... is that the kind of thing you're looking for?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these nagging thoughts and checking behaviors are significantly interfering with your daily life or causing you a lot of distress?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I wouldn't say they're causing a *lot* of distress. I really don't want to make it sound worse than it is, because I know there are people dealing with much harder things! \n\nI'm still getting through everything I need to do, you know? I'm managing my classroom, and I'm taking care of my aunt, and I'm keeping up with all my commitments. It's just... I guess it does interfere a *little* bit. Like I mentioned, my mornings definitely take longer than they probably should, and I hate that I sometimes get to school feeling all rushed and behind before the kids even get there. I always try to put on a happy face for them, though!\n\nAnd, um, my lesson prep and even just my evening journaling... they just don't feel quite as smooth or relaxing as they used to. It's just a little harder to wind down. But I'm still functioning fine, I promise! I just wanted to see if there was a way to make things a little easier. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not complaining too much!\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your checking behaviors are a way to prevent something bad from happening, even if it's just a small chance?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I suppose that's a good way to put it. I really don't want to sound like I'm paranoid or anything! I know the chances of something going wrong are super small. \n\nBut, um, yeah, when I go back over things—like checking my aunt's pill organizer, or making sure the stove is off, or looking through my lesson plans before I leave the classroom—it's mostly just to be absolutely sure everything is safe and taken care of. Or, you know, when I silently replay what I did in my head. I just want to prevent any little mistakes that could cause a problem for my aunt or my students. I'd feel just awful if something happened because I wasn't careful enough!\n\nSo I just do my little routine until I feel calm enough to move on. Even though, like I said, it does make me run late sometimes, which I'm really trying to work on! I just like to be responsible. I hope that doesn't sound too strange?\n\nDoctor: Do you have any thoughts that you feel compelled to think about over and over, even if you don't want to?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess so? I mean, I really don't want to make it sound like I'm, you know, losing my mind or anything! It's just... I guess that's actually kind of why I came in to see you. \n\nI just keep getting these... nagging thoughts. Like, I'll be driving to school, and suddenly my brain will just insist that I forgot something important. It'll be like, \"Did you leave your aunt's medicine out wrong?\" or \"Did you set up the classroom materials safely?\" And the thing is, I *know* I probably handled it perfectly fine. I'm usually very careful! \n\nBut the doubt just... it just keeps popping back into my head, even when I don't want to think about it. It's like a little loop that just won't turn off until I go through my checking routine. It's just a little annoying, honestly. I hope that's normal? Or at least, not too weird?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these nagging thoughts are unwanted and pop into your head without you intentionally thinking about them?\n\nPatient: Um, yes, exactly. They definitely just... pop up on their own. I really don't try to think about them! I'll just be driving to school, trying to plan out my day or listen to the radio, and out of nowhere, it's just, *bam*, \"Did you leave your aunt's medicine out wrong?\" or \"Did you set up the classroom materials safely?\" \n\nIt's completely unwanted. I really try to just push the thoughts away because, like I said, I know I probably handled everything fine. But the doubt just sneaks right back in, completely uninvited. I really wish they wouldn't do that, because it makes me feel like I'm not doing a good enough job, you know? I hope it doesn't make me sound like I'm a scattered person, because I really do try my best to be organized! Does that make sense how I'm explaining it?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel compelled to think about certain things in a specific way or repeat certain thoughts in your head?\n\nPatient: Um, well, I guess... yes, sometimes? I mean, I wouldn't say I'm obsessed with it or anything! I just... I like to be thorough. \n\nSo, um, yeah, before I leave the house in the morning, or before I lock up my classroom at the end of the day, I will kind of... go back over things. Like, I'll check my aunt's pill organizer a few times, or make sure the stove is off, or look through my lesson plans again. And if I can't physically check it, sometimes I just... silently replay what I did in my head, just to be sure I did it right. \n\nI just do it until I feel calm enough to move on, you know? It's really just a way to make sure everything is taken care of. I mean, I know I mentioned it makes me run a little late sometimes, which I'm definitely trying to fix! But it's just my way of being responsible. I hope that doesn't sound too strange or... or like I'm doing something wrong?\n\nDoctor: How much distress do these nagging thoughts and checking behaviors cause you on a daily basis?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I really don't want to make it sound like it's ruining my life or anything, because it's not! I'm still getting through work just fine, and I'm taking care of my aunt and all my family commitments. I'm still doing everything I'm supposed to do! \n\nIt's just... I guess it does cause a *little* bit of stress? Mostly just because my mornings take a bit longer with all the checking, so I sometimes arrive at school feeling a little rushed or, you know, slightly behind before the day even really starts. And... well, I guess my lesson prep and my evening journaling just don't feel quite as smooth or as relaxing as they used to be, because those thoughts kind of interrupt me. \n\nBut I'm really managing okay! I just wanted to see if there was a way to make things a little easier. I hope that's an okay reason to be here? I just want to make sure I'm doing the right thing by trying to fix it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to think about certain things in a specific order or sequence in your head?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess maybe a little bit? I know I brought this up a minute ago, about how I check things before I leave the house or my classroom. When I go back over those same things—you know, my aunt's pill organizer, the stove, my lesson plans—I do kind of like to do it in a certain routine. \n\nAnd... well, when I can't physically check and I have to silently replay what I did, I do kind of need to replay it from start to finish in my head in the right order. If I lose my place or think about it out of order, I usually just start over and do it a few times until I feel calm enough to actually move on. I know that's part of why it makes me run a little late sometimes, which I am really trying to be better about! I don't want to be an inconvenience to anyone. It's just my way of making sure I didn't miss a step. I hope that doesn't make me sound too rigid or anything... is it okay that I do it that way?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Um, well... I really try to! I mean, I try to just push them away and tell myself everything is fine. But... I guess it's actually kind of hard to just stop them completely.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi, doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me, I really appreciate it. \n\nUm... well, I came in because I've been having these... I hope it doesn't sound too silly, but I keep getting these nagging thoughts that I forgot something important. It's probably just normal stress, but, like, I'll suddenly worry that I left my aunt’s medicine out wrong, or that I didn't set up my classroom materials safely for my students. \n\nAnd even when I know I probably handled it just fine—because I try to be very careful!—the doubt just kind of keeps popping back into my head. Does that make sense? I just want to make sure I'm explaining it right for you.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself repeating certain actions over and over, like checking something multiple times?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I guess I do, a little bit? But I think it's mostly just me trying to be thorough, you know? Like, before I leave the house, or before I leave my classroom for the day, I’ll... I’ll go back over the same things a few times. Just checking the pill organizer, or making sure the stove is off, or looking over my lesson plans again. \n\nOr sometimes I just... silently replay what I did in my head, just to be absolutely sure. I really only do it until I feel calm enough to actually move on. It... well, it does make me run a little late sometimes, which I always feel so bad about. \n\nIs that the kind of thing you mean? I hope I'm answering your question right. I just really want to be responsible, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like checking or replaying these things in your head helps to reduce your worries or anxiety?\n\nPatient: Yeah, exactly. Um, like I was just saying, going back over things—you know, checking the stove, or the pill organizer, or doing that little silent replay in my head—it really does help. It's like... it just kind of turns the volume down on the worry? \n\nOnce I do it a few times, I finally feel calm enough to actually move on and walk out the door. I mean, I know I just mentioned that it makes me run late sometimes, which I'm really trying to work on because I absolutely hate inconveniencing my coworkers or my students! But... yeah, doing all that just helps me feel settled so I can focus on being a good teacher. \n\nI hope that's the kind of answer you were looking for? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Are these worries and checking behaviors affecting your relationships with others or your ability to teach and work effectively?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I wouldn't say they're ruining anything, you know? I'm definitely still getting through all my work commitments and taking care of my family just fine. I try really hard not to let it affect anyone else. \n\nBut... I guess, if I'm being completely honest, it does make my mornings take a lot longer than they probably should. I'll sometimes get to school feeling all rushed and... just kind of behind before the kids even get there, which I really hate. And, um, things like my lesson prep, or even just my journaling in the evening—which usually helps me unwind—they just don't feel quite as smooth or relaxing as they used to be. \n\nIt's nothing I can't handle, I promise! I'm still doing my best. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is... I just want to give you the whole picture.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to perform these checking behaviors or else something bad will happen?\n\nPatient: Um... I wouldn't say I think something *terrible* is going to happen, exactly. It's more just... well, like I was saying before, I just want to be really responsible, you know? \n\nSo, before I leave the house, or before I lock up my classroom, I'll just go back over those things a few times. You know, checking the pill organizer, making sure the stove is off, looking at my lesson plans... or just silently replaying what I did in my head. I just... I feel like I *have* to do it until I feel calm enough to actually move on. If I don't, I just feel this... this nagging feeling that I missed something important, and I wouldn't want to let anyone down because I wasn't careful enough. \n\nI know I mentioned it makes me run late sometimes, which I'm really trying to fix! But I just do it to be safe. Is that... is that normal? I hope that answers your question okay.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these checking behaviors are necessary to prevent something bad from happening to yourself or others?\n\nPatient: Um, I know I'm probably sounding like a broken record right now, I'm so sorry! But... well, no, I don't really think of it as preventing a huge disaster or someone getting hurt, exactly. It's really just... like I was saying, before I leave the house or my classroom, I'll just go back over those same things a few times. You know, checking the pill organizer, the stove, my lesson plans, or just silently replaying what I did. I just do it until I feel calm enough to move on. \n\nI know I keep mentioning that it makes me run late sometimes, and I promise I'm really trying to get better at that because I hate being an inconvenience to anyone! But it's mostly just about wanting to be a thorough, responsible person, not because I think someone is in actual danger. \n\nI hope it's okay that my answer is basically the same as before? I just want to make sure I'm answering you right and giving you exactly what you need.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any thoughts that keep repeating in your mind, even when you try to ignore them or think about something else?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess I do. It's actually kind of why I decided to come in and talk to someone. It's not like crazy voices or anything scary like that, I promise! It's just... these really nagging thoughts that I forgot something important. \n\nLike, I'll be driving to work and suddenly I just can't stop thinking, \"Did I leave my aunt's medicine out wrong?\" or \"Did I set up my classroom materials safely so the kids won't get hurt?\" And the thing is, I *know* I probably handled it. I'm usually so careful! But even when I try to just push it away and focus on my day, the doubt just... keeps popping back into my head. \n\nIt's a little embarrassing to admit that I can't just brush it off like a normal person would. I really try to ignore it, but it's just so persistent sometimes. Does that sound like what you were asking about? I hope I explained that okay.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel compelled to perform any specific rituals or behaviors in a specific order or manner?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess it kind of goes back to what I was saying before, about how I check things? I'm sorry to keep bringing up the same stuff, I hope that's okay! \n\nBut, um, I wouldn't call them \"rituals,\" really. That sounds so... I don't know, intense? It's just my little routine. Like, before I leave the house for work, or before I lock up my classroom at the end of the day, I just have this way I like to go over things. I'll check my aunt's pill organizer, make sure the stove is off, look over my lesson plans... or sometimes I just have to silently replay what I did in my head, just to be absolutely sure. \n\nI don't necessarily have a strict, rigid order I *have* to do them in, but I do feel like I need to keep checking or replaying it until I feel... settled, I guess? Until I feel calm enough to actually walk out the door. I know it's a little silly, especially since it makes me run late sometimes, which I feel terrible about! I really try not to let it affect my schedule. But I just like to be thorough. Is that... is that the kind of thing you're looking for?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these nagging thoughts and checking behaviors are significantly interfering with your daily life or causing you a lot of distress?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I wouldn't say they're causing a *lot* of distress. I really don't want to make it sound worse than it is, because I know there are people dealing with much harder things! \n\nI'm still getting through everything I need to do, you know? I'm managing my classroom, and I'm taking care of my aunt, and I'm keeping up with all my commitments. It's just... I guess it does interfere a *little* bit. Like I mentioned, my mornings definitely take longer than they probably should, and I hate that I sometimes get to school feeling all rushed and behind before the kids even get there. I always try to put on a happy face for them, though!\n\nAnd, um, my lesson prep and even just my evening journaling... they just don't feel quite as smooth or relaxing as they used to. It's just a little harder to wind down. But I'm still functioning fine, I promise! I just wanted to see if there was a way to make things a little easier. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not complaining too much!\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your checking behaviors are a way to prevent something bad from happening, even if it's just a small chance?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I suppose that's a good way to put it. I really don't want to sound like I'm paranoid or anything! I know the chances of something going wrong are super small. \n\nBut, um, yeah, when I go back over things—like checking my aunt's pill organizer, or making sure the stove is off, or looking through my lesson plans before I leave the classroom—it's mostly just to be absolutely sure everything is safe and taken care of. Or, you know, when I silently replay what I did in my head. I just want to prevent any little mistakes that could cause a problem for my aunt or my students. I'd feel just awful if something happened because I wasn't careful enough!\n\nSo I just do my little routine until I feel calm enough to move on. Even though, like I said, it does make me run late sometimes, which I'm really trying to work on! I just like to be responsible. I hope that doesn't sound too strange?\n\nDoctor: Do you have any thoughts that you feel compelled to think about over and over, even if you don't want to?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess so? I mean, I really don't want to make it sound like I'm, you know, losing my mind or anything! It's just... I guess that's actually kind of why I came in to see you. \n\nI just keep getting these... nagging thoughts. Like, I'll be driving to school, and suddenly my brain will just insist that I forgot something important. It'll be like, \"Did you leave your aunt's medicine out wrong?\" or \"Did you set up the classroom materials safely?\" And the thing is, I *know* I probably handled it perfectly fine. I'm usually very careful! \n\nBut the doubt just... it just keeps popping back into my head, even when I don't want to think about it. It's like a little loop that just won't turn off until I go through my checking routine. It's just a little annoying, honestly. I hope that's normal? Or at least, not too weird?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these nagging thoughts are unwanted and pop into your head without you intentionally thinking about them?\n\nPatient: Um, yes, exactly. They definitely just... pop up on their own. I really don't try to think about them! I'll just be driving to school, trying to plan out my day or listen to the radio, and out of nowhere, it's just, *bam*, \"Did you leave your aunt's medicine out wrong?\" or \"Did you set up the classroom materials safely?\" \n\nIt's completely unwanted. I really try to just push the thoughts away because, like I said, I know I probably handled everything fine. But the doubt just sneaks right back in, completely uninvited. I really wish they wouldn't do that, because it makes me feel like I'm not doing a good enough job, you know? I hope it doesn't make me sound like I'm a scattered person, because I really do try my best to be organized! Does that make sense how I'm explaining it?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel compelled to think about certain things in a specific way or repeat certain thoughts in your head?\n\nPatient: Um, well, I guess... yes, sometimes? I mean, I wouldn't say I'm obsessed with it or anything! I just... I like to be thorough. \n\nSo, um, yeah, before I leave the house in the morning, or before I lock up my classroom at the end of the day, I will kind of... go back over things. Like, I'll check my aunt's pill organizer a few times, or make sure the stove is off, or look through my lesson plans again. And if I can't physically check it, sometimes I just... silently replay what I did in my head, just to be sure I did it right. \n\nI just do it until I feel calm enough to move on, you know? It's really just a way to make sure everything is taken care of. I mean, I know I mentioned it makes me run a little late sometimes, which I'm definitely trying to fix! But it's just my way of being responsible. I hope that doesn't sound too strange or... or like I'm doing something wrong?\n\nDoctor: How much distress do these nagging thoughts and checking behaviors cause you on a daily basis?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I really don't want to make it sound like it's ruining my life or anything, because it's not! I'm still getting through work just fine, and I'm taking care of my aunt and all my family commitments. I'm still doing everything I'm supposed to do! \n\nIt's just... I guess it does cause a *little* bit of stress? Mostly just because my mornings take a bit longer with all the checking, so I sometimes arrive at school feeling a little rushed or, you know, slightly behind before the day even really starts. And... well, I guess my lesson prep and my evening journaling just don't feel quite as smooth or as relaxing as they used to be, because those thoughts kind of interrupt me. \n\nBut I'm really managing okay! I just wanted to see if there was a way to make things a little easier. I hope that's an okay reason to be here? I just want to make sure I'm doing the right thing by trying to fix it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to think about certain things in a specific order or sequence in your head?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess maybe a little bit? I know I brought this up a minute ago, about how I check things before I leave the house or my classroom. When I go back over those same things—you know, my aunt's pill organizer, the stove, my lesson plans—I do kind of like to do it in a certain routine. \n\nAnd... well, when I can't physically check and I have to silently replay what I did, I do kind of need to replay it from start to finish in my head in the right order. If I lose my place or think about it out of order, I usually just start over and do it a few times until I feel calm enough to actually move on. I know that's part of why it makes me run a little late sometimes, which I am really trying to be better about! I don't want to be an inconvenience to anyone. It's just my way of making sure I didn't miss a step. I hope that doesn't make me sound too rigid or anything... is it okay that I do it that way?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these nagging thoughts are forcing their way into your mind without you wanting them to?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I wouldn't say they *force* their way in, like I have no control over my own brain or anything! It's just... I guess they do kind of pop up even when I'm trying to focus on other things. \n\nLike, I might be driving or grading papers, and suddenly I just get this nagging feeling that I forgot something important. Like maybe I left my aunt's medicine out wrong, or... or I didn't set up the classroom materials safely for the kids. And even when I tell myself, \"No, I'm sure I handled it,\" the doubt just kind of... keeps coming back? \n\nI know it probably sounds silly, because I'm a very responsible person! I just want to make absolutely sure everyone is safe and taken care of. But yeah, those doubts do kind of show up when I'm not expecting them, and then I just have to do my little mental review to feel better. I hope that's a normal thing to experience? I just want to make sure I'm explaining it right.\n\nDoctor: Can you stop thinking about these nagging thoughts if you want to?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I really try to! I mean, I try to just push them away and tell myself everything is fine. But... I guess it's actually kind of hard to just stop them completely. \n\nLike I was saying before, those nagging thoughts about forgetting something important—like my aunt's medicine or the classroom materials—they just kind of keep popping back up. I'll tell myself, \"You handled it, don't worry about it,\" and I really *want* to just move on and grade my papers or whatever I'm doing. But the doubt just kind of sticks around until I go through my mental checklist. \n\nI'm really trying my best to just ignore them, I promise! I know I shouldn't let it bother me so much. Is it... is it bad that I can't just turn them off whenever I want? I really hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is, because I am still getting everything done!\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
